Maintenance and procedure of accounts.
As per rule-100 of ODA RULES -1983 the authority shall maintain cash book including subsidiary cash books for financial transaction . But it was seen that a bank
book has been maintained instead of cash book in main finance .  Further   individual cash book was maintained by each division separately.  Due to non
maintenance of main cash book the following information could not be worked  out .
1. Cash analysis of closing balance .
2. Reconciliation of bank accounts.
3. Details of advance position .
4. Position of fixed deposit.
5. Position of sweep account .

  a) Comments on Opening Balance
 
           As per A.R NO. 10699/AR/2013-14 PURI for the year 12-13 the O.B as on 1.04.2012 was taken from budget figure due to non conduct of audit of the previous
year.At that time the O.B/C.B of Bank Book of main finance were not worked out. But during course of audit for the year 2013-14, the authority produced a cash book
called bank book with O.B/C.B  calculation for smooth conduct of audit and reconciliation of figures . Hence the O.B of 403979409.85 was taken from the Bank Book
instead of  last audit report  C.B of RS 473171693.80 . 
The  reason of difference of C.B of Bank book and audit.-
        The difference of Rs 331688000.00 is due to the fixed deposit amounts shown as expenditure  in S.B.I Secry. branch in A/C no-30756039241 as well as Bank
book on 20.06.2013(Delt in para No -7 of this A.R). 
        The reasons of difference of the balance Rs.51241231.60 ( 382929231.60-331688000.000)   between audit figure and cash book in pass book position was
discussed in para-5 of this A.R .
 b) Comments on closing balance of Divisions
         On checking of the cash book of Divisions (I,II,III,IV & Hort. ) it is seen that only receipt and expenditure were booked . No closing balance is arrived in the cash
 7 / 150

[page 8]
 AUDIT REPORT 
24-06-2015
book with the pass book position. only the cash balance   is reflected in the cash book  at the end of the every month. Hence, the local authority is advised to arrive the
closing balance properly into cash book after everyday transaction .
Sweep A/C.
           Whenever the balance amount of an account exceeds a minimum specified amount , the surplus amount transferred to a separate amount at a higher interest
of rate called sweep a/c. When any cheque is  issued for payment if outstanding balance is insufficient than the sweep account automatically matured and credited to
the account along with the , interest to discharge the payment. During checking of pass books it was noticed that a large no. of sweep accounts bearing different
account nos. were created & matured. The main purpose is to earn more interest for the authority. But it was seen that the cash book was silent on sweep transfer on
that day. So the audit faced more difficult to reconcile the bank account. The authority is requested to maintain the cash book /bank book as per the transactions made
in the pass book by the bank towards sweep A/Cs during the year 2013-14 made available to audit is furnished below.

Reconciliation
Reconciliation of Bank a/c:- 
In pursuance of  F D OM NO:- 690/F dated- 21.01.2009 of principal secretary to Government Finance Department, it has been categorically specified that the local
authority must have to prepare reconciliation statement of discrepancy between cash book figure and pass book figures at the end of each month. But it has seen that
the local authority has failed to reconcile the discrepancy of cash book balances with that of pass book balance of main finance, Divisions at the end of the year
2013-14. In view of the above Government order, non-reconciliation of Bank account figure with reference to cash book figure is considered highly irregular. Inspite of
several verbal approaches and issue of objection Memo.No- 119/30.3.15, 112/26.3.15 the local authority failed to produce the reconcile statement of Main Finance/
Divisions. Due to non reflect of sweep transfer in Cash book/Bank book in every month it is  too difficult to reconcile the Bank passbooks. All the deposits &
withdrawals  were checked with reference to pass books. However, the local authority is requested to reconcile the bank pass book figure with cash book figure in
every month & compliance reported to audit. The difference amount of Rs. 306025712.84 between Cash book/ Bank book figure and  pass book figure as on
 11 / 150

[page 12]
 AUDIT REPORT 
24-06-2015
31.3.2014 is kept under objection.

1 Nil Nil Nil Nil 0.00 Nil The building material stock was not
purchased during the year under
audit.
 
Comments
Non conduct of physical verification of  movable  properties.
           As per chapter-VII rule-57 of Odisha Development Authorities rules-1983,physical verification of the stock of the movable properties shall be done once in
every year by such officer as they may be authorized by the vice-chairman in this behalf. But during checking of the stock register, the above rule was not followed by
the B D A . No physical verification was conducted by the authority in every year. The authority is requested to conduct the physical verification of the movable
properties of the authority in every year and necessary certificate may be endorsed in the stock register and compliance reported to audit.

Para-8.3- Advance outstanding more than one year
As required under Govt. order No-2221/F,XIV-AUD-II/2009,BBSR,Dtd.8th march 2002,any advance paid and remain outstanding for more than one year is to be
treated as unsecured advance and a loss to the auditee Institution. In spite of issue of audit objection statement(A O S p-36,109) regarding the adjustment of such
huge amount advance the local authority failed to comply.Hence such outstanding advance is suggested for recovery  from the officers who granted the same and the
payees in equal proportions in accordance with Finance Deptt.Order No-15179 dt.23.09.13.The abstract of such outstanding advance is given below.
1.Main Finance = 312036.00
2.Division-II        =      223.00
3.Division-III        =     750.00(adjusted vide Vr. No-80/30.5.14)
4.Horticulture Div.=143100.00(adjusted vide Vr No-18/30.10.14)
 Para-8.4- Important Irregularities noticed during checking of the advance position.
1. The advance ledger of   main finance was not properly maintained i.e the Vr.No/date, purpose of advance payment , Name of the Sanctioning Authority, adjustment
of advance  was not reflected in the  ledger.
2. Neither the advance ledger has been maintained in all the Divisions nor the purpose of advance mentioned in cash book of respective divisions.
3. As per the provisions of SR 509 of Odisha treasury code (volume-I) ,a second advance will not be granted unless the adjustment of  first advance. But in B D A   the
same rule was not followed.
4.  The advance payment was booked as final expenditure without showing it in the closing balance of cash book.
5. Adjustment of advance was not shown in bank book of main Finance. Every adjustment of advance   should be reflected in cash book/bank book of main finance .
    The above irregularities  need be rectified hence forth.
                  
 
 
Responsible Person for this paragraph

duly surrendered to Government immediately on expiry of the period of one year from the date of sanctioned.

alarming .Hence necessary steps may be taken for early utilization of the same by obtaining fresh sanction from the competent authority  or refunded the same to

  Pending  U.Cs

PARA: 13 AUDIT OF RECEIPTS
13.1 - Less collection of sanction fee (OSP-145-148_) planning Section
Ref-File No. BP2B-50/13
 Approval of building plan over plot no. 168/3082
Mz- Patrapada  in favour of Smt. Rasmita Naik.
          On scrutiny the above file it was appeared that permission under O.D.A Act’ 1982 was granted vide order No-44878/BDA dt. 16.11.13 for construction
of a stilt +three storey residential apartment building over plot no-168/3082, khata No-722/183, village-Patrapada in the development plan area B.D.A,BBSR
with the following parameters.
Stilt floor Area 450.72 sqmt
1st floor 432.59 sqmt
2ndfloor 432.59sqmt.
3rd floor 432.59sqmt.
FAR 1.634sqmt
PARKING 411.47 sqmt
TOTAL BUILT –UP AREA(EXCLUDING FAR&PARKING) 1748.49 Sqmt.
       According to the above parameters, the scrutiny fee of Rs. 9300.00 and sanction fee of Rs.87425.00 were deposited on 7.1.2013 and 5.10.2013
respectively.
         But on scrutiny the approved drawing attached in the file it was seen that the building plan was approved with the following parameters.
Stilt floor Area 463.06sqmt
1st floor 442.74sqmt
2ndfloor 442.74sqmt.
3rd floor 442.74sqmt.
FAR 1.662sqrmt
PARKING 445.69sqmt
Service area  13.43sqmt.
Total built –up area(EXCLUDING FAR,PARKING & SERVICE AREA) 1791.28 Sqmt.
 
 27 / 150

[page 28]
 AUDIT REPORT 
24-06-2015
         Hence it was ascertained that the approved drawing was differ with the plan order .As per drawing, Smt. Naik would have constructed the house, which is
irregular.
        Due to allowing excess built-up area OF 42.79 sqmt (1791.28-1748.49),B D A sustained a loss of Rs.2140.00(42.79x50.00) towards sanction fee which
may be recovered from Smt.Rasmita Naik .
      On issue of audit objection memo the local authority replied that notice has been issued vide Letter no 13775 dt. 15.5.2015 for deposit of Rs 2140.00 to Smt.
Rasmita Nayak .After recovery the fact may be intimated to audit for verification.However for such less collection the following staffs are held responsible.

13.2 - Utilisation of compounding fees. (OSP-185) Planning Section
 
        As per clause-3(v) of Odisha Notification no-2534 dated 31.12.07 and No.2390 dated 31.12.08 at least 50% of compounding fees collected
shall be utilized for development purpose by the development authority. In case of compounding fee collected   towards regularization of parking
deficit area in respect of commercial /mixed land use building the found so collected by the authority shall be utilized for development of parking
facilities at appropriate locations.
          As per general ledger, a sum of Rs.10916623.00 has been collected towards compounding fees during the year 2013-14. As per the
aforesaid guideline the utilization of at least 50% of the above collection i.e Rs.5458312.00 during the year 2013-014 should be made towards
development purposes. Hence, the details of such project taken up by the authority out of compounding fees may be furnished to audit. The
budgetary provision made for such projects, actual amount spent during the year 2013-14 along with connected records/files/M.B etc. were not
produced to audit for check.
             original audit objection memo was returned by the local authority with replay &quot; the said para does not relate to finance wing. Hence
the para may be dropped &quot;. Therefore the local authority is requested to complay  from the concerned section and produce the same before
the next audit for verification. Till than amount of Rs.5458312.00 is held under objection.

amount of Rs.17443600.00 has been deposited by BDA towards LIC Super Scheme in the year 2013-14. It was asked through objection memo
to ciarify why the employees of BDA get  both the benefit of EPF & LIC Superannuation Scheme.The details given below.
LIC Super Scheme

  Total 17443600  
 
In response to the objection statement issued in this regard the local authority has replied that as per E.P.F rule 12% of Pay + GP + D.A should have
been contributed towards Provident Fund. But in B.D.A there was ceiling limit for contribution of Rs. 6,500/- which is Pensionable wages. BDA
requested Provident Fund Authority to allow contribution of 12% over the Gross Salary. But Provident Fund Authority didn’t consider. This caused
huge loss to the employee of B.D.A. In order to compensate such loss the authority decided to introduce L.I.C Superannuation Scheme to extend
Pensionable benefit to its employees. Accordingly as per decision of the authority. L.I.C Superannuation Scheme has been implemented in this
organization. So according to the reply of the local authority, they consulted with provident fund to deposit 12% of Gross salary towards the
contribution of EPF. But as per the EPF rule,12% of Basic pay is contributed towards EPF . So it is not understood why the file was put up to accept
12% of Gross salary for the contribution towards EPF. There is no such provision of LIC Super scheme in Govt.So the reply is not satisfactory and
the objection stands on its own merit Hence an amount of Rs.17443600.00 is suggested for recovery. steps need be taken to recover the amount
from the person concerned and compliance reported to audit. As per section 124 of ODA Act 1982 the authority may with the previous approval of
the state Govt. frame regulations. Without prior permission from Govt. the authority can not give the benefit to his employees.

The points of objection are
A)    In support of service tax deposited by the agency ,service tax deposit receipt is not attached in the voucher.
B)     Service tax @12.36% of the bill amount i.e Rs. 1128489.00 comes to Rs. 139481.24 or say Rs. 139281.00. But instead of Rs. 139481.00 ,a

C)    As per Govt. order no 30/2012-service tax dt. 20.6.12 of ministry of Finance, Deptt. Of Revenue, Govt. of India Sl. no.-8 ,25% of the service tax 
should be paid by  the service provider and 75% of the service tax payable by the agency receiving the service. Hence 25% of Rs. 139481.00 comes
to Rs. 34870.25 or say Rs. 34870.00 excess has been paid to the agency.

                      Again further scrutiny of the above mentioned voucher it is seen that an office order no.5980/B.D.A,  Bhubaneswar OE-iii-265/12 dt.
17.2.14 is attached in which it is clearly mentioned that the above watch and ward amount includes service charge and excludes service tax.  The
order has been signed by Sr. Administrative officer ,B.D.A. Hence the payment bill and the office order contradictory to each other and the total
service tax paid of Rs. 162962.00 of the bill/voucher is paid in excess suggested for recovery. 
In response to the objection memo the local authority  was not returned  the original memo.  Hence,the objection holds good on its own merit. In
toto Rs 242411.00 (79449.00 +162962.00  ) is suggested for recovery. Steps may be taken to recover the amount and compliance reported to audit.

14.18 - Inadmissable  expenditure towards T.A (OSP  240, 241 )
On checking of T.A vouchers with ref. to bankbook of main finance for the year 2013-14 ,it is seen that a sum of Rs. 21591.00 has been shown
paid to Sky king, B.B.S.R towards supply of air ticket to Smt. Bandita  Mohapatra  A.T.P vide  vr no. 30 dt. 13.1.2014. The above mentioned
amount of Rs. 21591 /- was paid for performing journey from Bhubaneswar to Ahmedabad for attending vibrant Gujarat National summit on
Inclusive urban Development.
On detailed scrutiny of vouchers it is revealed that-
1.     Mrs Bandita Mohapatra A.T.P is drawing scale of Pay of RS. 23210/- +5400/- (GP).Hence she is not eligible to perform air travel.(L.No.
16638/F dt.2.4.11)
2.     The air tickets were booked from M/S Sky king, B.B.S.R instead of O.T.D.C (O.M NO. 28783/F dt. 2.7.2003 &quot; It has been decided that
all bookings for air travel in respect of entitled officers of state Govt. who travel on duty would be done through ODTC Ltd. and not through any
other travel agent. OTDC have agreed to provide a discount of 2% on the basic air fare in respect of tickets that are booked through them.&quot;)
3.     A T.A bill is attached in the voucher claiming the travel expenses in connection to the air journey she had performed on 16.10.13 from
 Bhubaneswar to Ahmedabad and on 19.10.13 from Ahmedabad to Bhubaneswar. But in support of the journey performed original boarding pass
is not attached. Again only one side ticket in support of journey performed from Bhubaneswar to Ahemadabad is attached. Neither returned
journey ticket nor boarding pass is made available in the voucher.
            Under above circumstances it concludes that she had not performed the journey and T.A claimed for the purpose is fictitious and needs
recovery from the person responsible.
In response to the objection raised the local authority was not returned  the original memo with reply till the close of audit. Hence the objection
holds good on its own merit . Thus the amount of Rs. 21591.00 need be recovered and compliance reported to audit.

14.24 - Inadmissible expenditure towards ref freshment for farewell meeting (OSP- 249 )
On checking of the vouchers with ref. to bankbook of main finance, it is found that a sum of Rs.112000.00 has been paid in advance ( detailed
attached ) to meet the farewell meeting of the retiring employees. Expenditure in farewell meeting should be contributory.In response to the
objection memo issued in this regard the local authority replied that &quot;As per decision taken in the 120th authority meeting held on 10.4.2012
vide agenda item no. 5/120 authority decided as a welfare measure to make a provision for an amount of Rs6000.00 only to make the expenses
for the farewell meeting to felicitate retired officer / employee of BDA.&quot; But there is no such Govt. guidelines for refreshment and gift
expenditure to be met from funds of local body/ govt. institution. This causes a loss to the local authority and cannot be admitted in audit and the
ex-post-fact approval of govt. in finance department is necessary for expenditure of Rs.112000.00 is held under objection.

set up in Annex-2 of the said agreement. Hence, the process in which way the  I F C has been selected and the reason of signing this agreement
maybe explained to audit.
There are many organizations inside our state / country to provide the services as provided by I F C (Annex-2) . Again , during course of audit of
records of different divisions of B.D.A ,made available to audit, it was noticed that the following organizations are providing services for
preparation of plan ,working drawings, survey, investigation and preparation of project proposals like roads,housing schemes etc. Apart from that
planning and monitoring unit (PM U) as also functioning in B.D.A for providing consultancy farms engaged during the period under audit for

4.     M/s P.S Designing cell
5.     M/S S.M Consultants etc.
Is there any competition bid/ tender was called to select the organization .
The agreement was signed to pay $75000 for the services in 3 instalments each within 30 calender days as follows-
1st installment -$25000 -Date of signature if the agreement.
2nd  installment -$25000 –mobilization of consultants.
3rd  installment -$25000 nalization of transaction structure.
             As per the agreement on 29.8.13, the BDA paid  1st installment of $25000 without any work of I F C . It is illegal and irregular. After doing
some work , the payment could have been made by way of changing the agreement in mode of payment of remuneration. The agreement was
made with IFC that full payment will be made in united states without any deductions i.e taxes, duties,charges.

fund. The scheme is introduced to cover all employees of BDA of their service period and uniform death benefits for all employees of BDA. L.I.C
of India has furnished the annual renewal premium demand notice a sum of Rs. 852998.00 only on dated 13.1.14 . After kind approval of

been produced to audit. The reply is not satisfactory. Till production of Govt. approval to next audit for verification, an amount of Rs.852998.00 is
kept under objection.
14.28 - Non Production of Records(OSP-234)
On checking the paid Vouchers for the month of March-14, it was seen that an amount of Rs. 6,10,308.00 was paid in favor of B D A Employees
Gratuity Fund Trust to deposit the renewal premium to LIC of India  P & G.S. Deptt. for the master Policy No- G G C A -31185 w.e.f 1.3.14 to
28.2.15 vide Vr. No-52/14.3.14.In response to the objection statement issued in this regards, the local authority has replied that in the 88th
Authority meeting held on 23.9.14 has decided that the service conditions and rules for the employees of BDA has not yet been approved by the
state Govt. As a result of which, the employees of BDA are deprived of getting post retirement benefits. In order to give such benefits, BDA has
agreed to implement the gratuity scheme of LIC and introduced the financial year-2001-02.L.I.C of India has furnished the annual renewal
premium demand notice a sum of Rs. 6,10,308/- . After kind approval of Vice-Chairman, BDA, the above premium has been paid to L.I.C for
renewal of gratuity scheme of BDA staff. The necessary documents are enclosed for ready reference . But any Govt. approval for the formation of
Gratuity Trust could not be produced to audit .The necessary Govt. approval need be accorded for the same and produced to next audit .Till then
an amount of Rs.6,10,308.00 is held under objection.

High court of Orissa.
  The judgment was passed on dt.4/25.1.12 and it has been ordered that the learned counsel for petitioner does not press the WP  ,hence the writ
petition is dismissed accordingly. As such Rs.5000.00 was sanctioned in favour of Sri Avin Chandra Deo,Advocate of BDA . But, according to
the decision taken in 111th authority meeting held on dt.03.07.09  the fees admissible to Sri Deo   is of Rs.500.00 only.
         In response to the audit objection statement issued in this regard,the Local Authority replied “It is not applicable to the Advocate conducting
the cases in High court of Orissa vide  this office order no-9702 dt.07.07.09 . In accordance with the decision taken in 111 th Authority meeting
vide item no-39”.But the said office order could not be produced to audit. 
    Since the case was not attended to by the petitioner for further redressal and the case was dismissed on its own merit,there is no genuineness
for payment of entire legal fees. As such Rs.4500.00(5000.00-500.00) is considered to be irregular and excess payment ,which needs recovery
from Sri Avin Chandra Deo,Advocate failing which the following persons  are held responsible. Steps may be taken to recover the amount and
compliance reported to audit.

14.40 - Excess Payment due to wrong calculation (OSP-164)
On checking the paid Vouchers w.r.t connected file no.- F&A-I-68/13,  it was noticed that vide office order No.-25441/BDA dt-28.6.13 the pay of
38 nos. of  employee of BDA(mate category) were stepped up their pay with the pay of Sri. Rabindra Pattnaik, mate Junior to them w.e.f 1.3.06
with pay 6210 and 1650. Acoordingly the arrear salary were paid as detailed below.
       (1)vide Vr.no.-128/30.7.13,the arrear salary for the period 3/06 to 5/13 was paid to 33 nos. of employees.
(2)Vide Vr.no.-67/16.8.13, the arrear salary for the period 3/06 to 5/13 was paid to 3 nos. of employees.
(3) Vide Vr. No.-37/8.10.13 the arrear salary for the period 3/06 to 5/13 was paid to Nityananda Behera, mate.
     On scrutiny of the file, it was noticed that the increment of the aforesaid employees sanctioned from 1.3.09 was Rs. 6960 +G.P.1650 (by
wrongly rounding off the pay ) instead of Rs.6950 as per Circular No.51387 dt 21.10.09. Accordingly the excess payment was made to them. The

PARA: 15 AUDIT ON WORKS
15.1 - Irregular found on checking of works file
           The main objective of B.D.A is to take up planned and systematic development of its areas. It prepares development plans including zonal
development plans, undertake works pertaining construction of housing colonies, shopping Centers, markets, industrial estates and provide
public amenities. It regulates development and uses of land including private land and undertakes schemes for improvement and clearance of
slums and re-development programmes. The authority receives grants from Govt. for construction of above civil works. These works were under
taken by the authority through contractors. Scrutiny of records revealed the following common irregularities.
(a) Due to improper maintenance of work files the audit faced many problems on checking of work bills. The work file contains tender details,
agreement, original estimate with administrative approval & technical sanction details were kept at Estimate sanction. The measurement books
are kept near the engineers at engineering section. The payment in shape of running bill form are kept in Account Section. The cashier kept the
bills in the voucher guard file. No duplicate bill is available in the concerned work file. If a duplicate running bill with payment order in the
concerned work file then the checking of work files with reference to measurement books will be clear, smooth & time saving. Hence the authority
is requested to give necessary instruction to the engineering sections & Account Section of divisions for keeping the records in proper and
compliance reported.
(b) Maintenance of register:- As per Rule – 64 of O.D.A rules – 1983 a register of works shall be maintained in such form as may be approved by
the Authority. It shall contain record of every original work or repair, showing the expenditure incurred in comparison with the estimates and such
other particulars as may be necessary. But such work register has been maintained in the divisions of work section. Hence, the true and fair
picture of the assets created under various schemes could not be ensured dues to non- maintenance of work register, the possibility of double
payment can not be ruled out. Steps may be taken to maintain the works register properly showing the details of the source of expenditure, dates
of commencement and completion of works and handing over the asset etc. The register should be reviewed periodically by the authority.
(c)  No lead statement is available in any of the work files.
    (d) No analysis of rate is available to substantiate the rate provide in the original estimate of connected work file.
      (e)As per tender call notice, agreement and scheme of rate, the cement cost will be determined by the average factory price of three
manufacture of cement inside the state. But during checking of files it was seen that no such procedure was followed. Due to allowing flat rate in
cement there is possibility of excess payment.
15.2 -  Inadmissible payment allowed towards Splitting of Work (osp-287-290) Division --II
          As per clause 3.5.24 of the Odisha Public Works Department (O.P.W.D) Code,the splitting up of the work into several parts at the time of
calling of tenders is a measure which should be justified by circumstances and must be in the interest of the work. It must not be resorted to with
a view to evade the operation of any prescribed limit . Clause 3.5.25 of P.W.D Code provides that care should be taken to see that portion of
work allotted to different contractors are of distinct nature and are sufficiently separated to make interference with supplies of materials and
labour impossible. Scrutiny the relevant records revealed that the work'  Const. of compound wall over Ac.80.00 dec. of Govt. land in Mouza Sampur ,Bhubaneswar'  
was split into four reaches, i.e, East side Portion, West side Portion, North side Portion & South Side Portion. No Justification was on the records
to split the work, on scrutiny the three numbers of files viz (East, North & South) paid during the year under audit 2013-14 found that-
1)     All the parameters  was followed by the authority for selection of contractors.
2)     The negotiable rates were differs from one contractor to another though the nature of the work was same.  
3) Reference to voucher no-        i)East side 16/20.08.13,
 73 / 150

[page 74]
 AUDIT REPORT 
24-06-2015
                                                    ii) North side 12/08.08.13
                                                   iii) south side 15/20.08.13
        The works were executed in one place and in one financial year. Hence there is no possibility of change of lead ,  rate of material, labour
rate . If the work was executed by one executing agency  though one tender call notice, then there  was possibilities of less expenditure. The
comparative statement among the 3 works paid as on 31.03.14 was furnished below for reference. The same may be confirmed to audit and
necessary  compliance may be reported. If one tender was called & one agency was executed then the B. D. A would have saved Rs. 25503.00
as detailed below:-

compliance reported to audit.

15.3 - Excess Payment towards cost of  M.S Rod. Div-II Osp:- (277-281)

On issue of objection memo executive engineer Div-ii replied that estimation of a particular work is framed taking different items and ingredients of
work component on tentative basis . The rate is taken for tender purpose and contractors quote their rate without going through the rates of different
items of the work as framed in the tender and the lowest quoted contractor has been selected for execution . Estimate of a particular work is
prepared basing on the prevailing schedule of rate .As the tender cost based on estimate cost, there will be no difference in rates. Instead of taking
the rates of SAIL ,tentative rates were taken in to consideration which can not be admitted in audit. Hence ,the objection holds good on its own
merit.Therefore, the excess amount of Rs. 18122.00 may be recovered from the executants and compliance reported. The following officers are
found  responsible for the above excess payment.

15.8 -  In admissible rate allowed for removing dust, debris & cleaning bushes. osp-(270 to 271) Div-III
Name of the Work:- Peripheral work of Kalyan Mandap  at K-7 Kalinga Nagar
E.C = 47040.00    File No:- Eng-III 1361(W) 13 year 2013-14.
                               Agt. Value – 46984.00 (Reach-1)
                               Agreement No – 224F2/2013-14
Commencement of work-15.10.2013
Completion of work-          21.10.2013
Exe- Sarojini Mohanty.
J.E – Jyoti Prakash Mishra,
1st R&F/B –    Vr. No- 9 /16.11.2013   Rs.46984.00
                               MB NO- 1281 P-12 to 13 &Page-28
(i)     In admissible rate allowed for removing dust, debris & cleaning bushes 
On verification of MB NO-1281 page-2,P-12 to13 and p-28, it was noticed that a sum of  Rs.46984.00 has been billed  @ 839.00 per hour
for 56hrs and paid to the contractor towards removing dust debris & cleaning buses/ leveling the ground with help of Hydraulic excavator of 1
cum bucket. But the M.B does not contain the areas of such clearance and simply mentioned that the hydraulic excavator was put to use
for 56hrs. In absence of any calculation about the area of clearances and the output of Hydraulic excavator the audit is unable to ascertain the
actual output and the area of clearance. In view of that the local authority was requested to confirm the fact and figure and produce the same to
audit for verification .  Failing which, why the amount will not be suggested for recovery, was asked for clarification.
          In response to audit objection memo the local authority stated “It was decided that ,the Hon’ble Chief Minister of odisha will inaugurate
the kalyani Madap on 1.9.2013 accordingly to prepare the peripheral area of Kalyani Madap and all the road side has to be cleared and level
properly to make access to this Mandap by the Hon’ble Chief Minister.
Accordingly one Hydraulic excavator was engaged to clean the area by cleaning the bushes and levelling the ground surface on hourly basis
and estimation accordingly was framed as per S.R. 2013.’
The reply of the local authority is imaginary   as  the work was started on 15.10.13 but the inauguration  function was before the
commencement of work. Hence the reply was not accepted and suggested for recovery. Thus Rs. 46984.00 may be recovered and compliance
reported to audit.

compliance reported.

(II):- Non- Utilization of Surplus earth on work.
A quantity of 456 cum of surplus earth was allowed the cost towards cost of surplus earth by mechanical means in Reach-2&3 as detailed below.
Reach – 2 - 240 cum
Reach – 3 – 216 cum
           TOTAL :- 456 cum
 A sum of Rs. 56544.00 @ 124.00 per cum   towards the cost of the quantity of 456 cum of earth was allowed to the contractor. As result of which
the carriage charges is treated as loss of BDA fund.
            In response to the objection statement   the Local Authority  replied “ The compacted earth surface quantity is 378.00 cum. Hence 456
cum after compaction comes to 378 cum which has been shifted by mechanical means to prepare the above roads. Accordingly the payment was
made for 456 cum @124.00/cum amounting to Rs.56444.00 only”. Without specification area in the estimate for the excavation of earth the
above amount cannot be  treated as genuine. Hence an amount of Rs.56544.00 is suggested for recovery.
 (iii) Non – realization the cost of royalty.

PPR was shown the execution in item- 2 of Reach - 3 . But the cost of royalty of Rs. 1556.00 was not realized from the bill as calculated below.
Hence, it needs recovery.
Quantity Done              Moorum Consumed
44.32  cum                  56.72 cum @ 1.28 cum *27.44 per cum=   
                                                                                           1556.39or1556.00
(iv) Signature Wanting:-
The original estimate was furnished in the file, which is not signed by the local authority. This may kindly be regularized and fact intimated to
audit.

production of the same may be ensured to next audit. Till then Rs. 52772190.00 is held under objection.

16.1 - Irregular expenditure towards refreshment(OSP 41,statement 1&2)
On checking of the vouchers with reference to cashbook of Division I and connected records it is noticed that  a sum of Rs 1,69,062.00 shown
as expenditure towards refreshment cost for meetings. This contravences the provision of F.D No 19917/F/dt. 03.05.2003the annual
monitary limit towards expenditure on refreshment to Rs 4000.00 Per annum incurred in the meetings convened by Addl. Development
Commissioner/ Secretaries/Spl. Secy./Addl. Secy. ., which states that refreshment expenditure shall be limited  to Rs4000.00 P.A and
secretary.,special secretary , Addl.secretary  ranked officers are entitled to this.Under which circumstances the expenditure have been made

16.4 - payment/ Clarification of Excess payment of service Tax (OSP 111&112)
On scrutiny of the vouchers with ref. to cash book of div-I it is found that a sum of Rs 2087199.00 was shown paid to S.M consultants towards
consulting services for preparation of detailed project report for construction of ring road in Bhubaneswar vide vr no. 6dt.6.1.201. Out
of Rs 2087199.00 , Rs 1857600.00 was paid towards consultancy  and Rs 229599.36 was paid towards service tax.
It is ascertain that B D A has planning branch, Architecture branch and engineering division in which a number of engineers, architects have
been engaged own resource personnels, B D A would have saved RS 2087199.00
          Again on further scrutiny of the voucher it is noticed that a sum of Rs229599.36 has been shown paid towards service tax over and
above the consultancy fees. But as per L.no .30/2012 service tax dt.20. 6.12 Ministry of finance(Rev. Deptt.), Govt. of India,
both service provider and service receipent is liable to pay tax at 50% each.(both has to bear the equal amount, point 9). Hence 50% of the
total service tax paid comes Rs 114799.68 say Rs114800.00 has been paid in excess needs recovery.
In response to the objection memo ,the local authority complied that ,” the engagement of consultant was a decision taken at higher level and
tender was called for and accepted by C .E –cum- E.M . Probably ,given the nature of work ,it was thought better in the interest of public to
engage a consultant.”
The reply submitted by the local authority is just shifting of responsibility.  Instead of submitting such compliance the local authority should have
clarified the matter from concerned  section and accordingly submit the compliance .
“Regarding payment of service tax, necessary correspondence is being made with the consultant to explain  as to why it had claimed 100%
service tax and why it will not be recovered from it. After necessary clarification ,appropriation action will be taken.”  The replay submitted by
the local authority is not at all convincing. Before  payment made to the agency such clarification should have been sought for. Hence the
excess payment  of Rs 114800.00 made to the agency violating the service tax rule no.30/2012 dt.20.6.12 needs recovery from the agency i.e
S.M Consultants failing which, the following persons were held responsible for such a loss
Ambica Prasad Mohanty .Ex. Eng                  
Gyana Ranjan Pati. DAO                                    
Prafulla Ku. Sahoo. J.E                                        
Pradeep Ku. Mohanty. A.E

(25% of Rs. 79669.00) has been paid in excess. The reason for such excess payment of Rs 19917.00 was asked through objection memo.

decision of higher authorities and agreement drawn by C.E- cum – E.M with the service provider. As it was felt that the J.Es in B. D. A were not

         It is ascertain that B D A has planning branch,  Architecture branch and engineering division in which a number of engineers, architects have
been engaged. If the detailed project report had been prepared by engaging own resource personnel/ technical personnel meant for same nature of
work, B D A would have saved Rs5480307.00 (3446500+5097330+36777).
On further scrutiny of the voucher it is seen that a sum of Rs 4012.67 has been paid towards service tax. But as per L no. 30/2012 service tax dated.
20.6.12 ,Ministry of  Finance (Rev. Dept.) ,Govt. of India, both service provider and service recipient is liable to pay tax at 50% each (I,e both has to
bear the equal amount,sl .no.-9) Hence 50% of the total service tax paid comes to Rs. 2006.33 or say 2006.00 has been paid in excess needs
recovery from P.S Design Cell.
In response to the objection issued the local authority replied that , “B D A is taking- up high raising housing projects in Bhubaneswar for affordable
Residential ,commercial & other nature of houses. For this sophisticated well equipped and well experienced Architectural designers have been
engaged to obtain consultancy and logistic services beginning from the survey investigation to completion of the housing projects . In the want of
such personals in B D A establishment for investigation of soil state , survey, investigation preparation of drawing ,design architectural modality of
the buildings ,estimation both physical and financial , preparation of R F P documents, supervision of works and quality assurance etc. required for
the completion of the projects . The consultancy firm I,e Design Forum has been paid with the fees of R. 51.00 lakhs for performing the above task
up to the execution of work as per their agreement for the work construction of affordable housing scheme over 7.00 Ac. Of land at Subudhipur at a
contract cost of Rs 1928492896.00 which is essential for the construction of the project in view of the flourishing township of capital
city, Bhubaneswar and present competitive market trend. The different branches functioning under B D A like planning, exclusively approves the
individual planning of housing projects of capital city, Jatani & Khordha, Engineering branch executing and implementing the existing and new works
of B D A and architectural branch is not well enforced , equipped and commensurative to the buildings now constructed to cater the public choice.
The consultancy fee so incurred is also very merge to the cost of the building and the benefit to be received after its completion.
The consultancy fees paid to the other firms like P.S Design cell for preparation of plan ,sectional drawings,3D views for construction of E W
S blocks at K-9 (B) and survey investigation ,preparation of drawings for the work K-6,phase –I ,Bhubaneswar (Transit house) are genuine
and indispensable.
Besides the above technical staff of B D A working in Planning and engineering are over loaded with their own assignment. Consultancy fee paid to
the outsourcing firm for obtaining supporting consultancy . Logistic and architectural is essential and unavoidable”.   it may be stated here that if the
staffs of B D A are not well enforced , equipped and commensurative to the buildings now constructed to cater the public choice, they should be
given training  from time to time so that they will be able to cater to the public choice and B D A could have saved Rs 5480307.00

 AUDIT REPORT

to be payable by the service recipient in the line of clause E of the above circular of service tax. However as per the work order/ agreement the
service tax has been released.”

II Excess Payment towards  Service Tax
       On further verification of the said vouchers, it was noticed that a sum of Rs. 410414.00 has been paid towards service tax over and above the
approval  charges of the project. But as per Letter no.- 30/2012 Service Tax dated 20.6.12 Ministry of finance (Department of Revenue) Govt. of
India, both service provider and service recipient are liable to pay tax 50% each. Thus, 50% of service tax amounting to Rs. 205207.00 (50% of
410414.00) was paid in excess towards service tax to the service provider by the BDA. Hence, it needs recovery.
  In response to the audit  objection memo no reply was furnished by the  Local Authority. Hence necessary steps need be taken to recover the
amount of Rs.205207.00 and compliance reported to audit.

17.5.13.&quot; It is to mention here that  the construction work of Khordha kalyan mandap with all its infrastructure was completed by 02/2011

Khordha municipality,  observing the inauguration function by spending Rs. 28940.00 is not admissible in audit . Hence, Rs.28940.00 is
suggested for recovery  from the official responsible . Steps may be taken to recover the amount and compliance reported to audit.

16.27 - Irregular expenditure towards lifting of garbage of DIV-IV(OSP-182-184)
 
 
In course of checking of te log book of the veicle,TATA Ace bearing no. OR-02-BB-8171 and OR-02-BC-3208 it was found that these vehicles
have been used for lifting of garbage from Indira Gandhi Park and Biju Patnaik Park. Further, it has been ascertained that for the general
cleaning and sanitation of I.G.Park for the period from dt. 02.05.12 to dt.01.05.13 agreement of contract has been made with
Sri Surendra Kumar Behera vide agreement no.H2-F2/12-13 by the Horticulture Division .That agreement of contract was extended
for 2months,i.e.,upto 01.07.13 vide proceeding of the meeting held on 20.8.13 in the chamber of the Chief Engineer-cum-Engineer
Member,BDA regarding approval of deviation and EOT for cleaning and sanitation of I.G.Park and Biju Patnaik park of BDA. For the period from
03.07.2013 to 02.07.2014 agreement contract was made with M/S Shivam Engineering, Sri Bikash Behera, Managing Partner ,
Plot no.-140,MIG-II,Kanan vihar ,BBSR,vide L.No.646/BDA,BBSR dt.06.07.13 of Chief Horticulturist, vide agreement no.14-F2-2013-14 for
general cleaning and sanitation of I.G.Park.
 
           Also it has been ascertained that for the general cleaning and sanitation of BijuPatnaik park,BDa,BBSR for the period from dt.02.07.13 to
01.05.13 agreement of contract has been made with Sri Surendra Kumar Behera vide agreement no.H-2-F-2/2012-13. Extended the agreement
up to 02.07.13 in the meeting held on dt.20.8.13 vide agreement no. Hort-1/F2-2012 for BijuPatnaik Park. For the period from dt.03.07.13 to
02.07.14 agreement of contract was made with Mahaveer multipurpose self help cooperative Ltd.,HIG-14,Phase-I,Kolathia,Khandagiri,BBSR and
work order for general cleaning and sanitation at BijuPatnaik Park,BDA,BBSR,vide letter no.643/BDA,BBSR/06.07.13 of Chief
Horticulturist,BDA,and agreement no.13-F2-H-2013-14. As per the condition of contract the agency must execute the work “General  cleaning of
park,sanitation of public toilet,disposal of garbage by mechanical means at municipal disposal point.” Hence, there is no need to lift garbage by
engaging own vehicle by BDA. Thus, the expenditure incurred towards  engagement of own vehicle for lifting garbage is a loss to the institution
when an agency has been engaged  for same puspose.

16.31 - Excess payment of service tax  of Div -IV(OSP-37)
 
 
        On scrutiny of the vrs. it is found that a sum of Rs.30683.00 has been shown paid towards service tax over and above  the remuneration
paid to the JEs ,provided by the G.A. Digital. But as per the Letter No. 30/2012-Service Tax dt.20.6.2012 of Ministry of Finance
(Revenue Deptt.),Govt. of India, both the service provider and service receipient is liable to pay tax at 25% and 75% rate respectively.
Hence,25% of the service tax paid by the service receipient,i.e.,Div-iv, BDA,i.e., Rs.7670.75 or say Rs.7671.00 (25% of Rs.30683.00) has been
paid in excess and needs  recovery from G.A.Digital Web World (P)Ltd.

16.32 - Inadmissible  payment of electricity duty charges of DIV-IV (OSP- 291)
 
 
On checking of vrs. w.r.t. cash book of Div –IV,BDA for the year 2013-14 it is found that a total amount of Rs. 1989504.00 has been paid
to CESU towards energy charges of BDA office building during the year 2013-14 ,which  include excess payment of  Rs. 98,278.00 towards
electricity duty charges (detail given in the statement given below) . Payment of  electricity duty for the BDA office building contradicted to the
section 13 of Odisha Electricity Duty Act.1961 (Electricity duty is not leviable or chargeable if it is consumed by Govt. offices ). Hence, this excess
payment of Rs. 98,278.00 towards electricity duty is inadmissible in audit and suggested for recovery from the official responsible .

24-06-2015

before 2013-14.

16.35 - Irregular approval of Building plan (OSP-149 TO 152) planning Section.
 
        Permission under sub-section (3) of the section-16 of the Orissa Development Authorities Act’1982(Orissa Act”1982) was hereby granted
in favour of M/S Shuvam construction Pvt. Ltd. For construction of a basement +stilt +nine multistory Residential Apartment building over plot
no-634,316,317,1455,632(p),314,315,318,319,320.325&326,Khata No.78.84,25,192,08,297,259,62 and 102, village-Nayapalli of BMC in the
development plan area of BBSR  with the total built up area of 24192.91Sqmt.having 153 Nos. of dwelling units vide no-7763/BDA dated 4.3.14
Initially, the owner of the M/S Shuvam Const. Pvt. Ltd. was applied for approval of the building plan without converting kisam from agricultural to
non-agricultural i.e Gharabari under section-8 of OLR Act. The DP/BP committee also not recommended for issue approval without submission of
documents with regard to conversion of land from agriculture to non-agriculture kisam was allowed on 26.3.13.
      But as per order no-48353/13.12.13 it was passed in 125th meeting of authority vide agenda No.-32/125 held on 29.10.13” required to submit
the conversion document of kisam to Gharabari prior to commencement of the building. The above permission will be applicable only for villages
where the settlement operation is still in progress”.
    As per the meeting of the Authority (Not as Act), on 20.12.13 the F.I reported that the settlement is operation in Mz-Nayapalli . Then the file
was processed and permission was granted with the following conditions.
i)                   In case the full plot or part thereof on which permission is accorded is agricultural Kisam, the  same may be convert to
non-agricultural Kisam  under section-8 of OLR Act before commencement of construction.
ii)                 The owner /applicant shall obtain NOC from  NAA/Environmental clearance from ministry of forest environment, Govt. of India and
submit to BDA wherever applicable before commencement of construction.
iii)               The owner /applicant shall get the structural plan and design by verified   the institutions identified by the authority for buildings more
 141 / 150

[page 142]
 AUDIT REPORT 
24-06-2015
than 30 meter height before commencement of construction.
         Scrutiny of the above file it was noticed that no certificate/ document from the settlement officer regarding settlement work was progress at
Nayapalli village during Dec’13 was attached in the file. Though a year has been passed, the settlement work would have been completed. Till
now the applicant has not submitted the conversion document or submit the receipt towards deposit of conversion charges. The fact may be
confirmed to audit.
  Again the permission has been granted without comply the points as cited above.  It is cleared that the 125th Authority meeting was supported to
the Builder for the approval of Building plan. Was there any provision for field verification to see whether the applicant was started the
construction work without submitting the above documents may be clarified to the audit and compliance reported .
In response to audit objection memo , the local authority has issued notice to M/S Shuvam Construction PVT LTD. vide letter no 15428 /BDA
29.5.2015 to submit the necessary documents required for approval.

PARA: 18 MISCELLANEOUS
18.1 - Mis-utilisation of Fund  by way of engaging excess staff for collection of parking fees.(osp-210)
 
  Scrutiny the parking fees of city bus stand W.R.T DCR for the year 2013-14. It was seen that a sum of Rs.211250.00  was collected towards
parking fees . Again it was ascertained  from the records  made available that eight numbers of staff of B.D.A  were engaged at city bus stand for
collection of parking fees. For salary,allowances and other incentives, a sum of Rs 1878198.00 was paid to the staff engaged in the bus stand
 143 / 150

[page 144]
 AUDIT REPORT 
24-06-2015
during the year 2013-14. Being an authority the  institution should try to increase the internal source of income by way of minimize expenditure.
But in this case a total sum  of Rs.1878198.00 was spent towards staff salary etc. engaged in the bus stand for collection of parking fees of
Rs.211250.00 only during the year 2013-14
                        Hence the vary  purpose of the authority to increase the income fails in collection of parking fees. There is nothing  but 
misutilisation of fund and man power . The figures may be confirmed to audit and it may please be stated was excess staff were engaged for
collection of parking fees though there was less amount collected.

18.2 - Annual Budget: (OSP- 133)
Preparation of budget is a statutory requirement. Expenditure without budgeted limits is illustrative of a very poor expenditure control mechanism.
          On 20.3.2013, the revised estimate for the year 2012-13 & Budget Estimate for the year 2013-14 of B.D.A was finalized vide agenda no. 3
of 124th Authority Meeting. The same was sent to the principal secretary to Govt., H&UD Dept.Odisha, BBSR Vide Lr. No.13692/15.4.13 for
approval. Again the revised estimate for the financial year 2012-13 & Budget Estimate for 2013-14 was approved by the Govt. in Housing &
Urban Development Dept. Vide Lr. No. 25040/HUD dt. 07.08.2013. The Budget of the B.D.A , was divided into two parts. i.e. one is Revenue and
another is capital. The Budget Estimate for the fiscal year 2013-14 was prepared with a justified revenue expenditure under different heads
including salary, pension contribution. The authority has put some emphasis on spending cut & augmentation of revenue receipt than that of
Financial Year 2011-12. For the Financial Year 2013-14, BDA has got short term roll plan on focusing certain projects like Group Housing at
Paikarapur, NH-5 to K-5 Junction road temporary transit shelter at C.S.PUR and Patrapada affordable Housing at K-4 and K-9(B), Kalinganagar,
BSUP Projects at Gadkan, residential and commercial complex at K-9(B),OD Terminal etc. BDA has also given priority for clearance of
outstanding land premium to G.A.Dept. and Revenue Authorities. Funds also have been provided to strengthen the enforcement activities of
BDA. Broadly, for the fiscal year 2013-14, the revenue receipt has been estimated at Rs. 4378.67 lakhs and its corresponding revenue
expenditure stands estimated at Rs.4321.32
18.3 - Non revocation of bank guarantees (osp-18)
On checking of available bank guarantee  statement  of  BDA with connected file it  was noticed that the validity of bank guarantees submitted to
the BDA by the  following developers in – lieu-of  Security deposit  for approval of building plan have already been expired. The detailed  list of
such developers along with bank guarantee value are given below. Neither the said   developers   submitted the extended  bank guarantee nor
the completion certificate of project .Further  the said invalid bank guarantees have not been revoked by the local Authority.
      As per ODA Act’1982, the BDA should do field   verification so as to ascertain whether the applicant has constructed the building  as per
approved plan or not. But during checking of connected files in no case the site verification has been done by the Local Authority.
   Further , as per clause-7(2) and 77 of the building standard regulation 2008 in case the construction are not as made as per approved plan
then the security deposit should be forfeited and deposited to the BDA account.      As the site verification has not been done by the BDA it can
 not be ascertained whether   building  is as per approved plan or not.
     In absence of extended bank guarantee and completion certificate of the project, If the said bank guarantees have been revoked and credited
to BDA account, the BDA would have been got interest on the total bank guarantee value  of Rs.17096186.00
 144 / 150

[page 145]

24-06-2015
  On issue of objection statement in this regard  , it was stated that the local authority has issued cancellation show cause notice of permission for
non extension of bank guarantee.However steps may please be taken to revoke the bank gurantee of same  to the BDA account and shown to
next audit ,till then Rs.17096186.00 is held under objection.

 147 / 150

[page 148]

  As per Rule – 6 of OTC Vol – I read with Rule – 4 of O .G. F .R stipulates that all moneys received/realized on behalf of Govt. should be
deposited in fall into treasury/ with the competent authority within 3 days of its receipt/realization. Retention of Govt. money/revenue outside the
treasury is irregular & not permissible.
     Scrutiny the cash books of divisions of B.D.A it was seen that amounts collected from the work bills towards Rayalty, Vat, IT, Cess etc. during
the year 2013-14 were deposited to Govt. a/c in the next month. Appropriate action need be taken to remedy the violation of rules as cited above.
